# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-14

An expanded open-ended search across creative AI, with detailed guides for crochet/papercraft, image editing, neural audio, mobile media, video agents and editable 3D, plus additional eligible screened discoveries retained in the searchable pending-profile appendix. Earlier screened entries receive complete guides where the evidence supports them; first guides are not claims of launches today. This is source/documentation review: no discovered application was installed or run. Software, model weights, third-party assets, paid services and proprietary hosts are distinguished. Every platform is researched equally; each entry reports its actual documented route and leaves unpublished requirements unknown. Quality labels follow evidence rather than stars or guide length. Current licensing, setup and availability follow-ups update eight existing library assessments while preserving historical editions. Verified repository aliases are excluded from new-tool counts. Primary-source web research supplements all 104 observed fields; inaccessible sources, limited paper reads and partial ecosystem coverage remain explicit. Verified collection: 913/913 repository queries attempted; 913 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 75 bounded/truncated. All 25 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Separate from successful search queries, 48 watchlist fetches and 3 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Invoke’s current documentation describes a self-hosted layer/canvas image workflow. Today’s Classic Image Editor guide scopes its actual neural selection/face tools separately from a planned assistant; neither interface polish nor an OSS label proves output quality. [Source 1](https://invoke.ai/) |
| Video, animation & film | Current OpenReelio, Kaestral and Phosphene pages describe different desktop/agent routes. Phosphene’s changelog now lists a November 14 release; a dated release is not automatically a significant creative upgrade. Today’s OpenMotion and script-based reel guides disclose external agents, rendering tools and services separately. [Source 1](https://openreelio.com/) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://phosphene.video/changelog/) |
| Audio, music & voice | OpenSuno’s site describes local YuE2 work, while the ZONOS3-Mini card and publisher page are accessible. The requested complete ZONOS3-Mini LICENSE still returns 404, so its metadata label is not substituted for a full grant. Lux and Antipode document very different neural-model and agent/DSP routes. [Source 1](https://opensuno.org/) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/raw/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source 4](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) |
| 3D, reconstruction & assets | SolidCraft’s site describes parametric CAD with MCP and distinguishes a release candidate that can break. Origami-3Dfolder-ai is separately reviewed for painted/folded meshes; a GLB export does not establish printable or dimensionally accurate geometry. [Source 1](https://solidcraft.io/) |
| Browser tools & web media | The WebLLM primary paper supplies context for in-browser inference. A browser interface alone does not establish local model execution, universal WebGPU support or a complete media creation workflow. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15803) |
| WebXR, VR & AR | Meta’s IWSDK article and Google’s Three-Live announcement describe AI-assisted spatial web creation. Authoring capability, model/service terms and actual headset/browser compatibility remain separate checks. [Source 1](https://developers.meta.com/vr/blog/accelerate-vr-development-with-ai-and-immersive-web-sdk/) [Source 2](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html) |
| Computational art & creative coding | Fragment’s current site documents conventional canvas/shader creation and export. Those features alone do not qualify a project as AI; today’s agent-driven diagram and procedural-audio tools are admitted for explicit model-assisted workflows. [Source 1](https://fragment.tools/) |
| Interactive, immersive & live media | ossia describes interactive installations, and LivePose documents camera inference streamed over OSC. Today’s hand-gesture application uses neural landmarks with conventional gesture/sprite rules; its screen effects are not a volumetric hologram. Morse offers a separately screened Gemini-to-short-haptic-message route; a participatory-art adaptation is a proposed use, not a completed performance. [Source 1](https://ossia.io/interactive-installations.html) [Source 2](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/) [Source 3](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/installation.html) [Source 4](https://github.com/Juan17la/Morse) |
| 3D printing & generative CAD | SOMA and Kerfline primary sites describe agent/CAD and image-to-laser-pattern routes. Generated geometry or SVG still needs material, kerf, dimensional and fabrication review; decorative output is not automatically machine-ready. [Source 1](https://somacad.app/) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/) |
| Games & production pipelines | ForgeAX’s documentation describes agent-led game authoring. LLM Gamebook remains an earlier screened framework while full backend/model configuration review continues; a generic chatbot or game description alone is insufficient evidence of an implemented creative tool. [Source 1](https://forgeax.github.io/docs/) |
| Motion capture & character animation | The Movens-Zero project page presents multimodal humanoid-motion research. Its demonstrations do not establish a full licensed application or direct export compatibility with a creator’s chosen rig. [Source 1](https://movens-zero.github.io/) |

Model-card availability and talking-head previews do not replace a complete software/weight grant or independent likeness/latency evaluation. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/avaturn-live/avtr-1/blob/main/README.md) |
| VFX, compositing & relighting | The Interactive Object Insertion with Differentiable Rendering primary paper was captured for research context. The separately inspected VFX Pipeline AI implementation is a prompt-to-code API helper; its broad README does not establish automatic Nuke/Maya graph execution or asset management. [Source 1](https://yaksoy.github.io/papers/SIG25p-LightAwareCompositing.pdf) |

[Source 1](https://seexpeng.github.io/data/papers/Neural%20Acoustic%20Fields%20for%20Mixed%20Reality.pdf) [Source 2](https://par.nsf.gov/biblio/10647880) |

Capture hardware, calibration, licensed implementation and downstream editing compatibility still need tool-specific evidence. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15719) |
| Editing, captions & post-production | Subtitle Edit’s current documentation includes AI-assisted subtitle workflows. Videoassist is reviewed for an optional transcript-to-plan model call; its silence cuts, crop and subtitle burning are conventional processing and do not generate a transcript. [Source 1](https://subtitleedit.github.io/subtitleedit/overview.html) |

Today’s embroidery/vector editors distinguish geometric editing, external agent control and model-backed art insertion rather than treating all vector operations as AI. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/SVGRender) |

Handwriting-style parameter extraction, a font preview and an actual exported usable typeface are different capabilities; DSH Scribble explicitly lacks TTF export. [Source 1](https://xk-huang.github.io/VecGlypher/assets/VecGlypher.pdf) |

SubSilicon’s completed guide covers branching stories with optional model-assisted writing, with export and model-account limits visible. [Source 1](https://hyyyyyyz.github.io/dramai/) [Source 2](https://hyyyyyyz.github.io/dramai/docs/) |

[Source 1](https://eurasip.org/Proceedings/Eusipco/Eusipco2021/pdfs/0000121.pdf) |
| Learned calligraphy and robotic brush performance | CalliRewrite’s author page describes recovering writing behavior from calligraphy images. Neural path inference, the physical robot/utensil controller and reproduction of a particular stroke quality need separate setup evidence. [Source 1](https://luoprojectpage.github.io/callirewrite/) |
| AI comic localization, relettering and translation | ComicTL’s current site describes local bubble detection/OCR and WebLLM translation. Browser/WebGPU capability and translation quality remain device/language-specific; no cloud-free claim is transferred to other comic tools. [Source 1](https://comictl.kiuyha.dev/index.html) |
| AI furniture, timber and joinery design | ShopPrentice’s site describes an authenticated MCP furniture-design service and paid AI credits. A public endpoint or free design mode does not by itself establish an open-source software grant or fabrication-safe joinery. [Source 1](https://shopprentice.com/) |
| AI game localization and playable translations | Taarib’s primary guide describes agent-assisted Arabic localization with gated stages. Package registries also expose game-localizer and gameloc; their license classifiers do not replace full grants or engine-specific patch testing. [Source 1](https://abdallah28r.github.io/taarib/en/) |
| AI stage lighting and show control | LacyLights’ current site describes theatrical control with optional AI integration. Generated cues still need fixture/patch/operator review; model assistance does not establish safe or reliable live-show behavior. [Source 1](https://lacylights.com/) |
| AI photobook, album sequencing and editorial layout | Music2P’s primary abstract covers multimodal album-cover creation. Album art is adjacent to photobook production, not evidence of an implemented pagination, print-proof or binding workflow. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01651) |
| AI seamless loops, sample design and DAW-ready stems | WAIvePulse’s site describes local song, separation, mixing and looping routes. Model restrictions, quantization and actual GPU fit must be checked per component; a “free/offline” banner does not resolve every model’s rights. [Source 1](https://waivepulse.com/) |
| Neural yarn modeling and machine knitting | The neural inverse-yarn primary abstract describes image-based procedural yarn inference. Today’s crochet tools instead interpret written instructions and verify counts; neither route proves physical gauge, fit or sewability. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.00154) |
| Neural watermarking for printed creative media | StegaStamp’s author page describes learned data embedding in physical photographs. A robust-decoding demonstration does not establish authenticity, provenance or resistance to every print/camera transformation. [Source 1](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp) |
| Learned halftones and neural print screening | The Deep Halftoning author page and CVF reversible-pattern paper were checked. Nikronic’s current source explicitly says paper numbers are not reproducible yet; its earlier screened entry is not promoted for having a paper or model components. [Source 1](https://nikronic.com/projects/deep-halftoning_project/) [Source 2](https://ttwong12.github.io/papers/invhalftone/invhalftone.pdf) [Source 3](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2021/html/Xia_Deep_Halftoning_With_Reversible_Binary_Pattern_ICCV_2021_paper.html) |
| AI brick sculpture and agent-assisted construction design | BrickGPT and BrickWright’s primary pages describe generated buildable brick structures. Physical stability claims require the specified parts/constraints; a rendered build is not proof that every generated structure can be assembled. [Source 1](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) |
| Neural optical holography and computational displays | Stanford/Princeton primary research and Odak/PADO documentation cover learned computer-generated holography. These require optical/display-specific modeling; ordinary parallax videos and glowing screen sprites are not treated as physical holography. [Source 1](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source 2](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/time-multiplexed-neural-holography/) [Source 3](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/artifact-resilient-holography/) [Source 4](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/odak/learn_models/) [Source 5](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/installation/) [Source 6](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/cgh/) [Source 7](https://cinescope-wkr.github.io/pado-hologram/) |
| AI poetry, kinetic literature and language installations | RiTa’s current page explicitly calls its library AI-free. It remains contextual creative-coding research rather than a new AI admission; J-space theater separately documents a concrete model-hidden-state visual-writing experiment. [Source 1](https://rednoise.org/rita/) [Source 2](https://jhave.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Cybefutu/j-space-theater) |
| Neural hair, fur and strand-groom authoring | Current HairGS, IPHG, StrandHead and HairGPT pages supply hair-generation/reconstruction context. A research/code link does not settle complete grants, release availability or downstream groom export; prior watchlist restrictions are not silently cleared. [Source 1](https://github.com/yimin-pan/hair-gs) [Source 2](https://xiaokunsun.github.io/StrandHead.github.io/) [Source 3](https://haiminluo.github.io/hairgpt/) [Source 4](https://github.com/facebookresearch/iphg) |
| Neural smoke, fluid capture and editable VFX fields | HyFluid’s author page and the PICT_smoke repository were checked; the separate PICT project-site URL returns 404. Smoke demonstrations do not substitute for full software grants or reproducible creator workflows. [Source 1](https://kovenyu.com/HyFluid/) [Source 2](https://19reborn.github.io/PICT_smoke/) [Source 3](https://github.com/19reborn/PICT_smoke) |
| AI pattern transcription and symbolic craft verification | StitchProof and Starlit connect model-assisted instruction interpretation with deterministic count verification. StitchProof discloses substantial synthetic false alarms and unresolved checkpoint access; Starlit’s optional API expands vocabulary rather than proving a physically correct garment. Qwen’s current model card is supporting context, not an end-to-end quality approval. [Source 1](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B) [Source 2](https://stitchfoundry.github.io/StitchFoundry/) |
| Neural rhythm tracking and synchronized visual media | BeatNet’s primary paper, current Rust package documentation and registry were checked. The selected BeatNet ports carry CC BY 4.0 software terms and Prism lacks a complete grant, so they are not admitted under the software-license policy. Separately MIT-licensed BEAST and Explore This are screened for neural rhythm extraction, without claiming a finished VJ integration. Fosfora is additionally profiled for its explicit learned vision bridges and depth route; ordinary audio FFT/shader reactivity is not the AI claim. The selected complete MIT alternative grant is distinguished from its modified Apache text. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.03576) [Source 2](https://docs.rs/crate/beatnet-rs/latest/source/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/bridges/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT) |

## Search and review counts

104 creative fields; 913/913 repository queries attempted; 25/25 model-task queries attempted. 61244 distinct source candidates and 1074 model leads. 40 detailed profiles and 61 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 75 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Fresh raw documentation/grants for JoyAI MLX, openedit and Xotion were captured, alongside live video-editor and video-generation topic listings. JoyAI/openedit are retained existing tools; Xotion’s full grant remains PolyForm Noncommercial and is excluded. Topic listings are discovery aids, not license or quality approval. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/video-generation?sort=stars_desc)
- **codeberg** · searched: Imaginer and Raspberry Pi diffusion project pages and Imaginer API metadata were accessible. Both broader diffusion/audio API searches returned 429 today. Those failed attempts were archived without bypass; this is partial ecosystem exploration and does not establish complete source or current license/setup coverage. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=diffusion&limit=25) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=audio&limit=25)
- **sourcehut** · searched: The current diffusion project search returned diffront, diffuser and prs-search. Diffuser’s complete MIT text and Cargo manifest were captured; the active dependency points to an author-local absolute path, leaving a portability gap. No unchanged retained entry was relisted as a new tool. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Current npm/PyPI/NuGet/crates metadata was researched without installing packages. TensorStack.Media now lists 0.14.10 while inspected source build properties say 0.14.9. crates.io identifies coral/beatnet-rs as the actual Rust project; its complete port grant/Cargo label is CC BY 4.0, so it is held under the software-license policy. Registry metadata is not a complete grant or quality review. [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/litert-ocr/latest) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/manimvox/0.2.0/json) [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json) [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/beatnet-rs) [Source](https://docs.rs/crate/beatnet-rs/latest/source/README.md)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Krita, SAT, Max/MSP and embroidery-host primary sources supplement plugin discovery. The TCSinger Max guide separates MIT adaptation code from proprietary Max and reproduced checkpoints. CoDesign’s full current launcher NOTICE is proprietary despite MIT manifests/docs; its existing listing receives an explicit correction rather than a new open-source feature. [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/installation.html) [Source](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp) [Source](https://github.com/imgly/codesign/blob/main/plugins/codesign/runtime/NOTICE)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh project documentation/gallery captures covered every effective field. New detailed guides include craft verification, embroidery, local media APIs and code-based audio/video. Some pages are app shells; 404, TLS, 525 and bounded initial PDF captures are recorded separately. Seven primary PDFs were subsequently downloaded completely and their first two pages extracted, not claimed as full-paper reading. [Source](https://stitchfoundry.app/) [Source](https://cristianonescu.github.io/OpenStitch/) [Source](https://xoliddev.github.io/demos/ai-video-studio/) [Source](https://antipode.quest/) [Source](https://open-design.ai/motion/)
- **research-code** · searched: Primary papers/project pages were read for neural acoustics, optical holography, fluid media, glyphs, craft and rhythm, alongside current repository source. BEAST and Explore This have complete MIT software grants and implemented neural rhythm code. Coming-soon code, paper-only claims and non-commercial software remain outside new admissions; publication prestige does not determine a quality tier. [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.17156) [Source](https://github.com/WildHoneyPie/BEAST) [Source](https://github.com/CPJKU/explore_this) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.03576) [Source](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source](https://kovenyu.com/HyFluid/)
- **international** · searched: Source review included Uzbek scripted-video production, Chinese comic/story/handwriting tools, German papercraft documentation and multilingual singing/translation contexts. AI routes were distinguished from default mocks or conventional helpers. Documentation language, geographic origin and popularity do not substitute for grants, implemented creative use or platform evidence. [Source](https://github.com/xoliddev/ai-video-studio) [Source](https://github.com/saber1037140885-design/manga-kit) [Source](https://github.com/junhan29/subsilicon-editor) [Source](https://github.com/yuanstarstar/dsh-scribble) [Source](https://github.com/malkreide/papercraft-ki)

## StitchProof

AI pattern transcription and symbolic craft verification · AI-assisted textile and computational craft · Creative learning & authoring · Creative publishing & presentation

The repository was created on 2026-10-17. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Review stitch-count arithmetic in permitted US-notation amigurumi or flat-row drafts, inspect each parsed row and export an HTML/Markdown review.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-14. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The repository provides an app video, screenshots and synthetic evaluations. Its headline fine-tuned run caught 43/45 seeded errors but also flagged 127/571 clean rows. A held-out “more times” notation caused 115/118 false alarms. These are author experiments, not real-designer or independent validation. [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.11+ and the app extras. The README starts a llama.cpp server with a base GGUF plus a separate LoRA, then FastAPI. Model preparation uses Tinker and the export extra; the displayed app/dev install does not include that optional extra. The exporter reads train/runs.json and requests its checkpoint through the authenticated Tinker account, so access to the author run is not established for another user. Reproduce/train a permitted run or use the clearly labeled recorded-answer demo while resolving this gap. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** MIT project code. Qwen and Gemma weight terms, llama.cpp/Ollama and Tinker access remain separate. The best LoRA download requires checkpoint access not established by a public source clone. Runtime can be local after the model files are available. Training/export may require separately authorized Tinker access and charges; the author’s past experiment cost is not a current price quote. [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Checks stitch arithmetic, not gauge, fit, appearance or a finished object. Synthetic-only training/evaluation and unsupported UK terms, lace, multi-size, short-row and Tunisian patterns limit applicability. Recorded answers are not a live model demo; checkpoint access and complete reproduction remain unresolved.

### How it uses AI

A fine-tuned Qwen3.5-4B translates crochet rows into structured stitch programs; ordinary Python simulates their counts and produces fixed explanations. Optional local Gemma 4 transcribes photos for correction before checking. [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

### Introduction

A fine-tuned Qwen3.5-4B translates crochet rows into structured stitch programs; ordinary Python simulates their counts and produces fixed explanations. Optional local Gemma 4 transcribes photos for correction before checking. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

### What it is good for

Review stitch-count arithmetic in permitted US-notation amigurumi or flat-row drafts, inspect each parsed row and export an HTML/Markdown review. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

### Demo & examples

The repository provides an app video, screenshots and synthetic evaluations. Its headline fine-tuned run caught 43/45 seeded errors but also flagged 127/571 clean rows. A held-out “more times” notation caused 115/118 false alarms. These are author experiments, not real-designer or independent validation. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

### Install

Use Python 3.11+ and the app extras. The README starts a llama.cpp server with a base GGUF plus a separate LoRA, then FastAPI. Model preparation uses Tinker and the export extra; the displayed app/dev install does not include that optional extra. The exporter reads train/runs.json and requests its checkpoint through the authenticated Tinker account, so access to the author run is not established for another user. Reproduce/train a permitted run or use the clearly labeled recorded-answer demo while resolving this gap. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.11+ and the app extras. The README starts a llama.cpp server with a base GGUF plus a separate LoRA, then FastAPI. Model preparation uses Tinker and the export extra; the displayed app/dev install does not include that optional extra. The exporter reads train/runs.json and requests its checkpoint through the authenticated Tinker account, so access to the author run is not established for another user. Reproduce/train a permitted run or use the clearly labeled recorded-answer demo while resolving this gap.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof.git
```


```sh
cd stitchproof
```


```sh
uv venv --python 3.11
```


```sh
uv pip install -e ".[app,dev,tinker]"
```


```sh
STITCHPROOF_BACKEND=replay python -m stitchproof.app.server
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

1. For the supplied demo, open localhost:7860 and select the sample; replay mode shows recorded model answers rather than live inference.
2. For new patterns, first establish access to the required base/adapter and start the documented llama-server on localhost:8080. Review the exported files and selected model terms.
3. Paste a small permitted draft, inspect the parsed programs and editable photo transcription, then review the numbers behind ERROR or CANNOT CHECK before exporting a report.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

- **Hardware:** Author measurement for one Q4 base/LoRA run: 2.93 GB model disk and 3.18 GB RAM on M4 MacBook; these are not universal minima or total setup space. Optional photo model and conversion/training files add resources. Numeric VRAM limits are not documented.
- **Software:** Python >=3.11; FastAPI/Uvicorn app extras; llama.cpp for live parsing. Tinker/tinker-cookbook are separate export/training extras. Node is needed only for frontend development. Ollama with gemma4:e4b-it-qat is optional for photos.
- **Platforms:** The documented inference/export example uses Metal on an M4 MacBook. A validated Windows/Linux/GPU support matrix is not documented; a browser frontend does not establish phone-local inference.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 4](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT project code. Qwen and Gemma weight terms, llama.cpp/Ollama and Tinker access remain separate. The best LoRA download requires checkpoint access not established by a public source clone.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project code. Qwen and Gemma weight terms, llama.cpp/Ollama and Tinker access remain separate. The best LoRA download requires checkpoint access not established by a public source clone.
- **Cost:** Runtime can be local after the model files are available. Training/export may require separately authorized Tinker access and charges; the author’s past experiment cost is not a current price quote.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

### Limitations

Checks stitch arithmetic, not gauge, fit, appearance or a finished object. Synthetic-only training/evaluation and unsupported UK terms, lace, multi-size, short-row and Tunisian patterns limit applicability. Recorded answers are not a live model demo; checkpoint access and complete reproduction remain unresolved. [Source 1](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof/blob/main/src/stitchproof/train/export.py)

### How it uses AI

### Install

The documented preferred route is the tiny Docker image, with an authorized Pexels API key supplied through the environment. Allocate at least 3 GB free RAM, two vCPUs and 5 GB disk; 4 GB RAM is recommended. The tiny route uses tiny.en Whisper, quantized Kokoro and low rendering concurrency. The command below expects the key already configured privately in your shell. [Source 1](https://github.com/gyoridavid/short-video-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gyoridavid/short-video-maker/blob/main/package.json)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. The documented preferred route is the tiny Docker image, with an authorized Pexels API key supplied through the environment. Allocate at least 3 GB free RAM, two vCPUs and 5 GB disk; 4 GB RAM is recommended. The tiny route uses tiny.en Whisper, quantized Kokoro and low rendering concurrency. The command below expects the key already configured privately in your shell.

```sh
docker run -it --rm --name short-video-maker -p 3123:3123 -e PEXELS_API_KEY gyoridavid/short-video-maker:latest-tiny
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/gyoridavid/short-video-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gyoridavid/short-video-maker/blob/main/package.json)

1. Visit http://localhost:3123 for the documented web UI; an older FAQ still says no UI, conflicting with its current Web UI section and packaged frontend.
2. Submit a short English scene with precise Pexels search terms through the UI or POST /api/short-video. Choose narration, caption placement, orientation and music level deliberately.
3. Poll the returned video ID status and retrieve the finished video through the documented API. Check speech, word alignment and footage/media rights before sharing; preserve outputs outside a disposable container when needed.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/gyoridavid/short-video-maker/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gyoridavid/short-video-maker/blob/main/package.json)

- **Hardware:** Documented general minima: 3 GB free RAM, two vCPUs and 5 GB disk; 4 GB RAM recommended. Dedicated VRAM is not required by the base route; larger models and rendering workloads need additional resources.
- **Software:** Docker for the preferred route; native npm instructions list Ubuntu 22.04+ with system libraries, or macOS with FFmpeg and Node tested on 22+. Native Windows is explicitly unsupported; WSL2/Docker resource configuration is discussed separately.
- **Platforms:** Linux and macOS native routes are documented; Windows requires assessing the separate container/WSL route. GPU acceleration is optional for Whisper and does not remove CPU-heavy Remotion rendering.

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers this application. Remotion has its own license; Whisper/Kokoro weights, FFmpeg build terms, background music and Pexels media/API permissions remain separate. The root MIT grant does not settle every commercial rendering scenario.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Reconciled arithmetic does not certify stitch interpretation, readability, garment fit or final physical appearance. Unknown notation stays UNVERIFIED. Enabling the API sends unfamiliar clauses to a remote provider; the local UI alone is not fully local AI inference.

### How it uses AI

An optional DeepSeek fallback translates unfamiliar crochet clauses into a small stitch-symbol vocabulary. The same deterministic stitch-count verifier accepts or rejects that proposal before PNG and A4 chart rendering. [Source](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/engine/llm_fallback.py) [Source](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Introduction

An optional DeepSeek fallback translates unfamiliar crochet clauses into a small stitch-symbol vocabulary. The same deterministic stitch-count verifier accepts or rejects that proposal before PNG and A4 chart rendering. ### Install

Create a Python 3.12+ environment, install requirements and Poppler tools, and start the local Flask app. Create an admin invitation with app/manage.py, redeem it locally and configure the documented authenticator. Add a private DeepSeek key only if the AI fallback is wanted; deterministic parsing works without it. [Source 1](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/engine/llm_fallback.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create a Python 3.12+ environment, install requirements and Poppler tools, and start the local Flask app. Create an admin invitation with app/manage.py, redeem it locally and configure the documented authenticator. Add a private DeepSeek key only if the AI fallback is wanted; deterministic parsing works without it.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit.git
```


```sh
cd starlit
```


```sh
python3 -m venv venv
```


```sh
./venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
./venv/bin/python app/manage.py invite "Local designer" --admin
```


```sh
./venv/bin/python app/app.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/engine/llm_fallback.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/ciberjohn/starlit/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Open localhost:6090, redeem the locally generated invitation and complete the account/authenticator setup.
2. Begin with sample/pattern.pdf or another PDF you may use. Enable the optional model fallback only after checking its provider configuration and data flow.
3. Inspect accepted symbols and every UNVERIFIED row; compare the PNG and A4 print sheets with the source before using them in a craft project.

## Origami 3Dfolder AI

AI origami, kirigami and paper engineering · 3D, reconstruction & assets · Games & production pipelines · AI agents for code-authored media production

The repository was created on 2026-11-01. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Create editable papercraft-style props or small game scenes from a shape spec and review an atlas, folding animation and multiple renders.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-14. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author supplies crate/tree/character examples and short scene videos. Supplied painted textures allow a no-new-generation folding example. Author scene frame rates and render timings describe particular machines/scenes, not independent quality or hardware minima. [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone the skill/source, install its Python requirements, and configure Blender 4.x; the author tested Blender 4.4. A permitted OpenAI image API account/key is needed for a new painting. Start with the supplied crate painting to inspect the deterministic fold/export path before making any separate paid request. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 software with NOTICE retained. Shipped example assets have a separate CC BY 4.0 grant. Image-model/provider terms and user input rights are independent. Local Blender/Python computation plus separately authorized image-generation charges for new paintings. Bundled painted examples do not require a new API request. [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Unfolding and folding use explicit geometry, not learned topology. The painter can alter scale and preserve unwanted fold lines; organic shapes remain limited. Brightness-derived normal maps are conventional estimates, not measured relief. Water clips require KHR_animation_pointer support or a separate engine-side implementation.

### How it uses AI

A coding-agent workflow unfolds simple mesh parts into UV dielines, asks an image model to paint those sheets, then folds them in Blender into textured low-poly GLBs with optional fold, face-expression and flow clips. [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)

### Introduction

A coding-agent workflow unfolds simple mesh parts into UV dielines, asks an image model to paint those sheets, then folds them in Blender into textured low-poly GLBs with optional fold, face-expression and flow clips. [Source 1](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source 4](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)

### What it is good for

Create editable papercraft-style props or small game scenes from a shape spec and review an atlas, folding animation and multiple renders. [Source 1](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source 4](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)

### Demo & examples

The author supplies crate/tree/character examples and short scene videos. Supplied painted textures allow a no-new-generation folding example. Author scene frame rates and render timings describe particular machines/scenes, not independent quality or hardware minima. [Source 1](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source 4](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)

### Install

Clone the skill/source, install its Python requirements, and configure Blender 4.x; the author tested Blender 4.4. A permitted OpenAI image API account/key is needed for a new painting. Start with the supplied crate painting to inspect the deterministic fold/export path before making any separate paid request. [Source 1](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source 4](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the skill/source, install its Python requirements, and configure Blender 4.x; the author tested Blender 4.4. A permitted OpenAI image API account/key is needed for a new painting. Start with the supplied crate painting to inspect the deterministic fold/export path before making any separate paid request.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai.git
```


```sh
cd Origami-3Dfolder-ai
```


```sh
python3 -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python3 scripts/template.py clean examples/crate/layout.json examples/crate/painted.png examples/crate/texture.png --review examples/crate/review/01
```


```sh
blender --background --factory-startup --python scripts/run_fold.py -- examples/crate review/01
```


```sh
python3 scripts/glb_check.py examples/crate/export/*.glb
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 3](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/examples/LICENSE) [Source 4](https://github.com/lejrimostfa/Origami-3Dfolder-ai/blob/main/scripts/image.py)

1. Review the supplied spec, layout and painting; make a copy for your own small prop rather than changing the geometry after it is painted.
2. For a new atlas, review the image request and model terms, generate it separately, then inspect cleanup/gate files and the four review views.
3. Open the GLB in the destination engine and check its Fold clip, materials, alpha and any animation extensions. Physical foldability or printing readiness is not established by a successful GLB export.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The Ollama assistant is planned, so this guide concerns delivered segmentation/landmarks. Small/profile/occluded faces and difficult touching-person masks require review; landmark warping is not a generative identity model. First-use downloads and native release signing are separate from local inference.

### How it uses AI

MediaPipe on-device body-part segmentation and face landmarks support person/part masks, face-aware liquify and a landmark-warp face-swap workflow in a layered raster editor. The separate Ollama conversational assistant remains on the roadmap. The first neural operation offers a version-pinned, SHA-256-checked model/runtime download; offline machines can load the four model files manually from a permitted source.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/mjonrdgcms/classic-image-editor.git
```


```sh
cd classic-image-editor
```


```sh
npm install
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/mjonrdgcms/classic-image-editor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/mjonrdgcms/classic-image-editor/blob/main/package.json) [Source 3](https://github.com/mjonrdgcms/classic-image-editor/blob/main/INSTALL.md)

1. Open a permitted photo and use Select > People to choose an individual and relevant body parts.
2. Preview the mask on contrasting backgrounds, refine its edge and send it to a new layer mask for correction.
3. For a face-aware edit, inspect landmarks, occlusion and seams; retain the editable layer/mask and compare with the original before export.

Current documentation shows an author-run picture-to-strawberry workflow and a Windows circle-edit example. Its 12-task assistant scores and compatibility harness are author evidence, not independent creative-quality or physical machine validation. [Source 1](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.12.md)

### Install

Use an official alpha desktop package or build with stable Rust and the documented native prerequisites. Start the editor, open View > Show/Hide > Assistant and configure a permitted provider/local model/agent. The browser preview currently lacks the Assistant and MCP controls, so it is not the AI route described here. [Source 1](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.12.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use an official alpha desktop package or build with stable Rust and the documented native prerequisites. Start the editor, open View > Show/Hide > Assistant and configure a permitted provider/local model/agent. The browser preview currently lacks the Assistant and MCP controls, so it is not the AI route described here.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch.git
```


```sh
cd OpenStitch
```


```sh
cargo build --release
```


```sh
cargo run --release -p openstitch-app
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.12.md)

1. Choose the intended hoop/machine profile and open a small original design or permitted image.
2. Configure the desktop Assistant and ask for one measured change or a simple badge. Inspect the preview, dimensions and Check Design findings; use the message-level undo if needed.
3. Review file/machine permission prompts and export only after checking thread, stitching and format. Keep a physical test sew-out separate from the app’s simulated checks.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/cristianonescu/OpenStitch/blob/main/docs/releases/v0.1.0-alpha.12.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Stable Rust with the workspace’s 2024 edition for source use. Windows needs the MSVC C++ build tools; Linux runtime needs X11/Wayland, libxkbcommon and graphics drivers. Model/API hosts and their resource needs are separate from the editor.
- **Platforms:** Developer-documented desktop routes cover macOS Apple Silicon/Intel, Windows x86-64 and Linux x86-64; a Flatpak workflow is also described. Browser editing is a reduced route without the Assistant. Numeric system RAM/VRAM and total disk minima are not documented.

## BackgroundRemoval RVM desktop wrapper

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · VFX, compositing & relighting

The repository was created on 2026-10-26. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prepare an editable video cut-out for compositing and compare edge quality across short clips before queuing longer footage.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-14. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author provides an interface screenshot and documents 4K/DirectML processing and multiple background modes. No independent 4K throughput, temporal-edge or memory benchmark was established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/matting_engine.py) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/video_processor.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the Windows-oriented Python 3.10+ source route, install requirements and launch app_gui.py or main.py. The source loads RobustVideoMatting through Torch Hub at first use; that fetch is additional executable model code/weights, not part of the wrapper’s MIT grant. PyTorch/DirectML packages are not fully version-pinned in requirements, so record a working resolved environment. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. - **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** MIT wrapper code. Dynamically loaded RobustVideoMatting code and checkpoints, PyTorch, FFmpeg and dependencies have separate terms; the selected upstream revision and weights need their own review. Local compute and storage; the scoped route does not document a mandatory paid generation API. [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/matting_engine.py) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/video_processor.py)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Temporal recurrence and guided matting do not prove universal clean edges. Exact upstream model version, complete dependency permissions and cross-platform reproduction remain unresolved. ProRes 4444 alpha export should not be described as lossless RGB video.

### How it uses AI

A desktop/CLI wrapper runs RobustVideoMatting with recurrent frame states, then composes video backgrounds or exports an alpha-bearing ProRes 4444 file. The interface exposes inference downsampling and matte cleanup. [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/matting_engine.py) [Source](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/video_processor.py)

### Introduction

The source loads RobustVideoMatting through Torch Hub at first use; that fetch is additional executable model code/weights, not part of the wrapper’s MIT grant. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/matting_engine.py) [Source 4](https://github.com/adriangbustos/BackgroundRemoval/blob/main/src/video_processor.py)

1. Begin with a short permitted clip and select transparent or a simple contrasting background.
2. Adjust the internal downsample ratio and matte cleanup, then inspect motion, hair edges and semi-transparent areas over more than one frame.
3. Check alpha interpretation and playback in the intended compositor before processing a batch. A selectable 4K export is not a guarantee of flicker-free mattes or adequate VRAM on every GPU.

## RoMa dense image matching

Photogrammetry, scanning & neural rendering · Images & design · 3D, reconstruction & assets · Photography, restoration & color

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Develop image-alignment, capture and photogrammetry experiments, inspect warped image pairs and reject bad correspondences before a separate reconstruction stage.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-14. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The repository supplies warp/matching demos and links its CVPR 2024 evaluation. The Tiny RoMa comparison is a separate variant; its table is not a score for full RoMa or proof that a particular reconstruction will be accurate. [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use an isolated Python environment; the README reports testing on Linux/Python 3.12 and offers uv sync or installation of romatch. The source declares Python >=3.9 and Torch >=2.5.1. Begin with the bundled Toronto demo, resolving the chosen model downloads and optional acceleration separately. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** MIT covers the authors’ code except the separately Apache-2.0 DINOv2 component. Selected checkpoint, dataset and upstream feature-model terms must be reviewed separately; the complete runtime stack is not one MIT grant. Local model inference/storage; no mandatory paid generation service is documented. Training and dataset preparation are separate workloads. [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Current code differs from the paper-era setup in scaling, Torch versions and a matrix decomposition. Sampling and RANSAC are stochastic. Heavy matching can motivate Tiny RoMa, but variant results and hardware requirements must not be transferred automatically.

### How it uses AI

RoMa estimates dense image-to-image correspondences and certainty maps using learned features and geometric matching, with sampling and conversion to pixel-coordinate keypoints for downstream estimation. [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)

### Introduction

RoMa estimates dense image-to-image correspondences and certainty maps using learned features and geometric matching, with sampling and conversion to pixel-coordinate keypoints for downstream estimation. [Source 1](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)

### What it is good for

Develop image-alignment, capture and photogrammetry experiments, inspect warped image pairs and reject bad correspondences before a separate reconstruction stage. [Source 1](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)

### Demo & examples

The repository supplies warp/matching demos and links its CVPR 2024 evaluation. The Tiny RoMa comparison is a separate variant; its table is not a score for full RoMa or proof that a particular reconstruction will be accurate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)

### Install

Use an isolated Python environment; the README reports testing on Linux/Python 3.12 and offers uv sync or installation of romatch. The source declares Python >=3.9 and Torch >=2.5.1. Begin with the bundled Toronto demo, resolving the chosen model downloads and optional acceleration separately. [Source 1](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use an isolated Python environment; the README reports testing on Linux/Python 3.12 and offers uv sync or installation of romatch. The source declares Python >=3.9 and Torch >=2.5.1. Begin with the bundled Toronto demo, resolving the chosen model downloads and optional acceleration separately.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa.git
```


```sh
cd RoMa
```


```sh
uv sync
```


```sh
uv run python demo/demo_match.py --im_A_path assets/toronto_A.jpg --im_B_path assets/toronto_B.jpg --save_path demo/roma_warp_toronto.jpg
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/pyproject.toml) [Source 3](https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa/blob/main/demo/demo_match.py)

1. Run the documented sample only after preparing the model/environment, then compare the two source images with the certainty-weighted warp.
2. Use the API to sample correspondences and convert normalized coordinates to pixels; inspect confidence and geometric inliers before estimating a transform.
3. For owned capture imagery, vary resolution/thresholds and compare failures. Dense matches alone do not establish measured scale, a complete mesh or fabrication-ready geometry.

- **Platforms:** Documented testing is Linux/Python 3.12. The demo contains an MPS branch and CPU/CUDA selection, but independent Mac/Windows validation is not established. Minimum RAM, VRAM, storage and exact GPU capability are not documented.

### How it uses AI

### Introduction

Open-source MCP connectors let a model client show text, slides, images, video and small HTML dashboards in the separately licensed TeleQ Android TV app, then request permitted screen snapshots for layout correction. ### What it is good for

Prototype assistant-authored presentation panels or exhibition display layouts with explicit TV pairing and visible human control. [Source 1](https://github.com/max4et/teleq-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/max4et/teleq-ai/blob/main/mcp/README.md)

### Install

Install/open TeleQ separately on an Android TV. On a computer with Node >=18.17 on the same Wi-Fi, run the documented pairing command, enter the code displayed on that TV, then register the stdio MCP command in the chosen model client. Web/phone clients use a different OAuth/cloud relay route. [Source 1](https://github.com/max4et/teleq-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/max4et/teleq-ai/blob/main/mcp/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install/open TeleQ separately on an Android TV. On a computer with Node >=18.17 on the same Wi-Fi, run the documented pairing command, enter the code displayed on that TV, then register the stdio MCP command in the chosen model client. Web/phone clients use a different OAuth/cloud relay route.

```sh
npx teleq-mcp pair
```


```sh
claude mcp add teleq -- npx -y teleq-mcp
```


```sh
npx teleq-mcp status
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/max4et/teleq-ai/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/max4et/teleq-ai/blob/main/mcp/README.md)

1. On the TV, choose Linked devices > Link an assistant, then pair the intended computer using the displayed short-lived code.
2. Ask the connected model for a small slide or visual panel using permitted assets; allow or deny snapshots on the TV and inspect the result.
3. Review model/layout errors and stop the assistant’s content with Back on the remote when needed. Unlink/revoke the intended assistant before reusing a shared display.

Audio, music & voice · Interactive, immersive & live media · Computational art & creative coding

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Compose short synthetic vocal phrases and edit note timing or pitch contours in a Max patch or Max for Live device, with separately permitted voice and score inputs.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-14. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/LICENSE.txt)

- **Reproducible setup · documented:** For inference, use Apple Silicon/macOS 14+ and Max 9+ with neural.gen~ installed. Download the exported model/tokenizer files from the linked model repository into export/max, or use the device download button. Open the bundled TCSinger2_UI.amxd or Max patches. Training and re-export are separate Linux/CUDA or Mac/Python workflows; they are not needed merely to load the provided export. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. The reproduced checkpoints, GTSinger/M4Singer/Opencpop/OpenSinger datasets, FLAN-T5/vocoder and upstream components require separate terms. ### How it uses AI

An unofficial MIT adaptation runs a reproduced TCSinger 2 singing model through neural.gen~ and ExecuTorch/MLX inside Max. Notes, lyrics, a style caption and an editable pitch curve become synthesized singing. [Source](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/export/max/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/shuoyang-zheng/TCSinger-2-MaxMSP) [Source](https://aaronz345.github.io/TCSinger2Demo/)

### Introduction

An unofficial MIT adaptation runs a reproduced TCSinger 2 singing model through neural.gen~ and ExecuTorch/MLX inside Max. Notes, lyrics, a style caption and an editable pitch curve become synthesized singing. [Source 1](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/export/max/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shuoyang-zheng/TCSinger-2-MaxMSP) [Source 4](https://aaronz345.github.io/TCSinger2Demo/)

### What it is good for

Compose short synthetic vocal phrases and edit note timing or pitch contours in a Max patch or Max for Live device, with separately permitted voice and score inputs. [Source 1](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/export/max/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shuoyang-zheng/TCSinger-2-MaxMSP) [Source 4](https://aaronz345.github.io/TCSinger2Demo/)

### Demo & examples

The repository links a Max interface and the original TCSinger 2 demo. Its exported checkpoint is a reproduction trained on four named singing datasets, not the official original checkpoint. Published research comparisons do not independently validate this export or its real-time reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/export/max/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shuoyang-zheng/TCSinger-2-MaxMSP) [Source 4](https://aaronz345.github.io/TCSinger2Demo/)

### Install

For inference, use Apple Silicon/macOS 14+ and Max 9+ with neural.gen~ installed. Download the exported model/tokenizer files from the linked model repository into export/max, or use the device download button. Open the bundled TCSinger2_UI.amxd or Max patches. Training and re-export are separate Linux/CUDA or Mac/Python workflows; they are not needed merely to load the provided export. [Source 1](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/export/max/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shuoyang-zheng/TCSinger-2-MaxMSP) [Source 4](https://aaronz345.github.io/TCSinger2Demo/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For inference, use Apple Silicon/macOS 14+ and Max 9+ with neural.gen~ installed. Download the exported model/tokenizer files from the linked model repository into export/max, or use the device download button. Open the bundled TCSinger2_UI.amxd or Max patches. Training and re-export are separate Linux/CUDA or Mac/Python workflows; they are not needed merely to load the provided export.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp.git
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp/blob/main/export/max/README.md) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/shuoyang-zheng/TCSinger-2-MaxMSP) [Source 4](https://aaronz345.github.io/TCSinger2Demo/)

1. Open the device/patch with the exported files available to neural.gen~ and confirm both score2f0 and sing load.
2. Enter notes and lyrics in the piano roll or read a JSON score; set tempo explicitly before importing a score because the piano roll retains its current tempo.
3. Generate a short phrase, listen for pronunciation and timing errors, then edit the F0 curve and compare. More export sampling steps increase latency; do not assume a live performance deadline is guaranteed.

Hook uses non-commercial SheetSage2/MERT components. Optional separators and Vibe have their own model grants; verify the linked primary terms for the exact chosen revision.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers Lux code. YuE2 weights/VAE have CC BY-NC 4.0 base terms with a separately described individual-creator permission; that is not a blanket company commercial license. Hook uses non-commercial SheetSage2/MERT components. Optional separators and Vibe have their own model grants; verify the linked primary terms for the exact chosen revision.

### Why it merits attention

### Install

In a Kotlin Multiplatform project, enable Maven Central and select com.llamatik:core with a concrete compatible published version. The 2.0 documentation replaces the old com.llamatik:library artifact. Supply a separately licensed model file, initialize StableDiffusionBridge and generate RGBA pixels, or use WhisperBridge for the documented 16 kHz mono WAV path. The developer must supply image display/save or video encoding. [Source 1](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/LICENSE-assets) [Source 3](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/NOTICE)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. In a Kotlin Multiplatform project, enable Maven Central and select com.llamatik:core with a concrete compatible published version. The 2.0 documentation replaces the old com.llamatik:library artifact. Supply a separately licensed model file, initialize StableDiffusionBridge and generate RGBA pixels, or use WhisperBridge for the documented 16 kHz mono WAV path. The developer must supply image display/save or video encoding.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/LICENSE-assets) [Source 3](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/NOTICE)

1. Add only the needed core module and resolve the documented model path for the intended platform.
2. Initialize the image model once, call txt2img or img2img on an appropriate worker, then check for an empty failure result and convert RGBA into the application’s image representation.
3. Call supportsVideoGeneration before requesting clips. Video output is a packed header and RGBA frames, not a finished MP4. Release native resources and measure memory/latency on the actual target.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/LICENSE-assets) [Source 3](https://github.com/llamatik/Llamatik/blob/main/NOTICE)

- **Software:** Kotlin Multiplatform/Gradle with Maven Central. Documented platform baselines: iOS 16.6+, Android API 26+, desktop JVM 21+, or a WebAssembly-capable browser. Native backends and features differ; video additionally requires a compatible loaded model/runtime.
- **Platforms:** The project offers Android, iOS, desktop and WASM interfaces, but that does not establish identical acceleration or support for every media operation. Numeric RAM, VRAM, model storage and full feature-by-platform validation are not documented in the reviewed guide.

### How it uses AI

Add edge_veda ^2.4.0 to the Flutter application dependencies and follow its Flutter quickstart. Source builds require macOS/Xcode 15+, Flutter 3.16+ and CMake 3.21+. The repository documents building the XCFramework and opening flutter/example with flutter run; choose and review the required model before using a media tab. [Source 1](https://github.com/ramanujammv1988/edge-veda/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ramanujammv1988/edge-veda/blob/main/flutter/QUICKSTART.md)

The repository documents building the XCFramework and opening flutter/example with flutter run; choose and review the required model before using a media tab.

```sh
git clone --recurse-submodules https://github.com/ramanujammv1988/edge-veda.git
```


```sh
cd edge-veda
```

./scripts/build-ios.sh --clean --release
```

cd flutter/example

```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ramanujammv1988/edge-veda/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ramanujammv1988/edge-veda/blob/main/flutter/QUICKSTART.md)

1. Prepare Apple code signing/device permissions and deploy the example to the intended iPhone.
2. Select the model matching the intended capability, then try a short image prompt or a brief owned microphone/camera sample.
3. Inspect the generated result and runtime traces, memory and thermal changes. A correct template/model pairing matters; do not treat the Android scaffold as a tested release.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ramanujammv1988/edge-veda/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ramanujammv1988/edge-veda/blob/main/flutter/QUICKSTART.md)

- **Hardware:** The reported 512×512 four-step image test used about 2.3 GB loaded memory on one A16/6 GB iPhone. Model/runtime memory and download size are distinct; numeric minimum RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not established.
- **Software:** macOS/Xcode 15+ for building; Flutter 3.16+ and CMake 3.21+. The example uses a native XCFramework, compatible model artifacts and physical-device permissions. Simulator CPU mode lacks microphone support in the documented status.
- **Platforms:** iOS/Metal is the supported validated route. Android is scaffolded and awaiting validation; Vulkan is planned. Maintainer measurements used an A16 iPhone with 6 GB RAM/iOS 26.2.1. These are observations, not minimum RAM/OS specifications; total storage and universal memory minima are not documented.

```sh

```

2. Adjust silence padding/thresholds against a short sample so breaths or word endings are not removed.
