Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Landmark inference is neural; gesture classification and sprite rendering are conventional. A camera feed with glowing wireframes is not a physical holographic display. Author test scripts do not replace an independent usability or installation review.

### How it uses AI

A webcam application uses MediaPipe neural hand landmarks, geometric gesture rules and OpenCV rendering to switch and animate screen-based sci-fi visual objects. [Source](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/jarvis/modelfetch.py)

### Introduction

A webcam application uses MediaPipe neural hand landmarks, geometric gesture rules and OpenCV rendering to switch and animate screen-based sci-fi visual objects. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/jarvis/modelfetch.py)

### What it is good for

Prototype a gesture-controlled screen artwork or workshop interaction using transparent PNG sprites and visible hand-tracking feedback. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/jarvis/modelfetch.py)

### Demo & examples

The README describes palm, fist, pinch and one-finger gestures selecting animated visuals. These are composited screen effects, not volumetric holograms, and no independent recognition-rate or latency result was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/jarvis/modelfetch.py)

### Install

Use Python 3.10–3.13, preferably the documented 3.12, a webcam and an isolated environment. Install requirements.txt and start main.py. Windows and POSIX launchers also prepare the environment and fetch the MediaPipe hand model; review those separately before use. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/jarvis/modelfetch.py)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.10–3.13, preferably the documented 3.12, a webcam and an isolated environment. Install requirements.txt and start main.py. Windows and POSIX launchers also prepare the environment and fetch the MediaPipe hand model; review those separately before use.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture.git
```


```sh
cd hand-gesture
```


```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python main.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/jarvis/modelfetch.py)

1. Activate the created environment using the command appropriate to the OS before the pip/start commands, allow camera access and try one hand under steady light.
2. Compare each gesture with its displayed classification; use D for debugging, H for the HUD and S to save a screenshot.
3. Replace the documented sprite PNGs with permitted artwork and inspect the interaction on the actual camera/display. Performance controls and smoothing do not guarantee recognition across users.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/requirements.txt) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture/blob/main/jarvis/modelfetch.py)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.10–3.13, MediaPipe >=0.10.30,<1.0, OpenCV >=4.8, NumPy >=1.24 and Pillow >=10. Optional pyttsx3 enables spoken feedback. Initial package/model download needs connectivity.
- **Platforms:** The README states Windows 10/11, macOS and Linux. This review did not execute those routes. A webcam is required; minimum CPU, RAM, VRAM and total storage are not documented. The hand-model artifact is about 8 MB, not the complete installation size.

2. Install the plugin in a compatible Claude Code host using the documented marketplace steps. Prepare Node 18+, Python 3 with NumPy/SciPy and Blender 4.2+ for the 3D style. The template can also be used without the agent; optional hosted voice generation has separate network access.

```sh
claude plugin marketplace add Dhananjay-alt/product-ad-video-skill
```


```sh
claude plugin install product-ad-video@product-ad-video-skill
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Dhananjay-alt/product-ad-video-skill/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.remotion.dev/license) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/hexgrad/Kokoro-82M)

1. Provide an original logo, permitted copy, product facts and an aspect ratio; inspect the generated brief and ad.json before rendering.
2. Choose apple3d, wrapped, terminal or minimal. For 3D, convert logo strokes/text to filled SVG paths and inspect framing before a full frame sequence.
3. Review narration, claims, timing and sound by playing the render, then export the MP4 and retain the project source. Numeric level checks alone do not establish a good mix.

### Install

Prepare Python 3.10+, Node 20+, FFmpeg and an installed Chrome used by puppeteer-core. Install the repository dependencies, configure one authorized Gemini API credential privately and set CHROME_PATH for the actual Chrome installation. The README’s Tungi Taksi episode documents voice, assembly, mix and rendering as distinct steps. [Source 1](https://github.com/xoliddev/ai-video-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xoliddev.github.io/demos/ai-video-studio/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare Python 3.10+, Node 20+, FFmpeg and an installed Chrome used by puppeteer-core. Install the repository dependencies, configure one authorized Gemini API credential privately and set CHROME_PATH for the actual Chrome installation. The README’s Tungi Taksi episode documents voice, assembly, mix and rendering as distinct steps.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/xoliddev/ai-video-studio.git
```


```sh
cd ai-video-studio
```


```sh
npm install
```


```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
cd uzbek-audio-series/tungi-taksi-ep1
```


```sh
python voice.py
```


```sh
python assemble.py
```


```sh
python mix.py
```


```sh
cd ..
```


```sh
node shared/render.js tungi-taksi-ep1 stills 5,40,120
```


```sh
node shared/render.js tungi-taksi-ep1 video
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/xoliddev/ai-video-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://xoliddev.github.io/demos/ai-video-studio/)

1. Review and adapt the permitted script and character lines before generating speech; verify model access and the configured Chrome path.
2. Generate and measure takes, then inspect assembled timing and the final audio mix. Correct pronunciation, truncation and unwanted spoken instructions manually.
3. Render representative stills first and play a short encoded segment before the full reel. Supply separately permitted music if desired; the documented basic route can use a silent music bed.

### How it uses AI

An AI coding agent turns a sound brief into a structured recipe; an Apache-2.0 Python/MCP instrument renders that recipe locally with procedural DSP and exports a WAV. 2. Install uv and Git, then use the documented host plugin/extension or clone the repository for its CLI. uv prepares the locked Python runtime; the project declares Python >=3.11 and host launchers prepare 3.12. ```sh

```sh
cd antipode-audio
```

```sh
uv run --locked antipode generate first-light.wav --preset chime --duration 2
```


```sh
uv run --locked antipode render examples/recipes/event-horizon.json event-horizon.wav
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. 1. For natural-language design, connect the documented local MCP/skill to a supported model host and start with a short sound brief.
2. Inspect the proposed layers, duration, stereo position and level; render to a new filename and listen at a suitable playback level.
3. Compare revisions using distinct filenames and the analysis tool. Retain recipe, seed and runtime revision; analysis measures signal properties, not artistic success.

Use Node 18+ and npm 9+, clone the repository, install its dependencies and run the Vite web development command. Electron development is a separate documented command. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/junhan29/subsilicon-editor/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/junhan29/subsilicon-editor/blob/main/package.json)

1. Create a short sequence with a choice and two outcomes, then add characters, a scene and permitted audio.
2. Use a configured model for a small passage or scene description and review consistency, intent and unsupported claims before accepting it.
3. Preview every branch and export a trial HTML/ZIP/EPUB/script through the intended route. Check assets, conditions and audio in the exported copy before distribution.

- **Platforms:** The README names macOS, Windows and Linux. This source review did not test those routes or establish an exact browser/Electron support matrix. Minimum RAM, CPU/GPU, VRAM and storage are not documented.

The repository was created on 2026-11-13. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Preserve and search handwritten creative notebooks while retaining original stroke backups and clearly marking machine-generated text.

- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Windows 10/11 with Samsung Notes installed/syncing and Obsidian desktop >=1.11.4 installed with installer >=1.9.12. Install/enable Memo Bridge through the documented community-plugin route or review its release files manually. Optional transcription is off until a provider is chosen; a local vision model or separately billed API is required. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. Samsung Notes and Obsidian are separately licensed hosts, and chosen models/APIs retain their own terms. Plugin use follows GPL. Required host/account access, local model compute and optional API requests remain separate; no current complete price comparison was established. [Source](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Import is one-way and depends on a reverse-engineered format. Calligraphy pen pressure, pencil texture, backgrounds and some old multi-page notes are imperfect or unavailable. A local import is conventional; optional model transcription may be local or remote and is not automatically accurate.

### How it uses AI

A GPL Obsidian plugin imports Samsung Notes handwriting as SVG and typed text as Markdown, with optional vision-model transcription and drawing descriptions for search and accessibility. [Source](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A GPL Obsidian plugin imports Samsung Notes handwriting as SVG and typed text as Markdown, with optional vision-model transcription and drawing descriptions for search and accessibility. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Preserve and search handwritten creative notebooks while retaining original stroke backups and clearly marking machine-generated text. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The author reports handwriting-error measurements on 21 pages of personal notes. Those small first-party examples do not establish general OCR quality; the source explicitly lists pen-rendering and older-note limitations. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Windows 10/11 with Samsung Notes installed/syncing and Obsidian desktop >=1.11.4 installed with installer >=1.9.12. Install/enable Memo Bridge through the documented community-plugin route or review its release files manually. Optional transcription is off until a provider is chosen; a local vision model or separately billed API is required. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Windows 10/11 with Samsung Notes installed/syncing and Obsidian desktop >=1.11.4 installed with installer >=1.9.12. Install/enable Memo Bridge through the documented community-plugin route or review its release files manually. Optional transcription is off until a provider is chosen; a local vision model or separately billed API is required.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Choose the Samsung data location and a target vault folder; start with a limited date range and inspect imported SVG/Markdown against the source.
2. Use the backfill dry-run before importing older notes. Keep original .sdocx versions and notice thumbnail-only or missing-page cases.
3. Enable a chosen vision provider explicitly, transcribe a small note and correct recognition/alt-text errors. Preserve the generated-text provenance and separate enrichment files when the note was already edited.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Windows 10/11, the syncing Samsung Notes Windows app, Obsidian desktop >=1.11.4 and installer >=1.9.12/Electron >=37.3. A separately configured vision model is needed only for AI transcription/description.
- **Platforms:** Windows desktop only; not mobile. The suggested local Qwen vision artifact is about 6 GB and optional GLM-OCR about 3 GB, not minimum system memory or full disk usage. CPU/GPU, RAM/VRAM and complete storage minima are not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** GPL-3.0
- **Weights:** The full GPL-3.0-or-later software grant was reviewed; bundled sdocx is GPL and xmldom MIT. Samsung Notes and Obsidian are separately licensed hosts, and chosen models/APIs retain their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. The full GPL-3.0-or-later software grant was reviewed; bundled sdocx is GPL and xmldom MIT. Samsung Notes and Obsidian are separately licensed hosts, and chosen models/APIs retain their own terms.
- **Cost:** Plugin use follows GPL. Required host/account access, local model compute and optional API requests remain separate; no current complete price comparison was established.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Import is one-way and depends on a reverse-engineered format. Calligraphy pen pressure, pencil texture, backgrounds and some old multi-page notes are imperfect or unavailable. A local import is conventional; optional model transcription may be local or remote and is not automatically accurate. [Source 1](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/natemiller82/obsidian-memo-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

An MIT React app optionally asks a local Llama vision model for approximate character body-part boxes, converts them into editable 3D primitives and derives row-by-row crochet instructions. ### How it uses AI

### Introduction

An MIT JavaScript map-animation framework is explicitly designed for AI agents to author short scene files, obtain real geospatial data and render deterministic 2D/2.5D map videos. [Source 1](https://github.com/MusicMaster4/map-bamba/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

### Install

Prepare Node 20+, FFmpeg on PATH and Chrome/Edge or a compatible Chromium installation. Clone and install dependencies, run the documented environment doctor, then scaffold a scene and inspect its contact sheet before rendering. A separately configured coding agent supplies the AI authoring; the renderer itself is conventional. [Source 1](https://github.com/MusicMaster4/map-bamba/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare Node 20+, FFmpeg on PATH and Chrome/Edge or a compatible Chromium installation. Clone and install dependencies, run the documented environment doctor, then scaffold a scene and inspect its contact sheet before rendering. A separately configured coding agent supplies the AI authoring; the renderer itself is conventional.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/MusicMaster4/map-bamba.git
```


```sh
cd map-bamba
```


```sh
npm install
```


```sh
node bin/bamba.js doctor
```


```sh
node bin/bamba.js new my-scene
```


```sh
node bin/bamba.js sheet scenes/my-scene.js
```


```sh
node bin/bamba.js render scenes/my-scene.js
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/MusicMaster4/map-bamba/blob/main/README.md)

1. Give the model a permitted place/story brief and inspect the resulting scene’s locations, dates, labels and motion choices.
2. Check the geocoded stops, route, map-data coverage and attribution; preview representative frames and correct unsupported factual claims.
3. Render a short trial and retain its source/cache for revision. Community tile, routing and lookup services require considerate use; cached re-rendering is different from an offline first acquisition.

- **Platforms:** Author testing used Windows 11 with an NVIDIA GPU; Linux/macOS share a less-tested code path. Numeric RAM, VRAM and total storage minima are not documented. A reported RTX 3070 render time is an observation, not a minimum or speed guarantee.

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers framework code. OpenStreetMap/OpenFreeMap/OSRM/Nominatim/Overpass data have separate attribution/use conditions; bundled fonts are OFL and terrain/example datasets have their own terms. Keep the renderer’s OpenStreetMap credit.
- **Cost:** Local rendering plus separately arranged agent access. Community geospatial services are described as free for light use; heavy production may require self-hosted or paid infrastructure.

- **Creative results · documented:** The source describes analysis stages and encoding paths, not independent highlight-selection or engagement results. Optional model ranking can fall back to signal ranking on failure; a completed job does not prove its clips are the best moments. A clip export does not grant permission to repost another creator’s footage. Local transcription/rendering with optional separately billed hosted ranking; no required application subscription is documented. [Source](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Facecam framing is beta and optional ranking is fallible. A local renderer does not make hosted ranking private/offline. Minimum VRAM and a guaranteed processing rate are not documented; large recordings require more storage than the startup threshold.

### How it uses AI

An MIT local Rust/web application uses Whisper transcription, signal-based moment detection and optional language-model ranking to make captioned vertical clips from gameplay recordings. [Source](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

### Introduction

An MIT local Rust/web application uses Whisper transcription, signal-based moment detection and optional language-model ranking to make captioned vertical clips from gameplay recordings. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

### What it is good for

Review candidate highlights from permitted gameplay, adjust framing and export short-form edits without a mandatory cloud renderer. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

### Demo & examples

The source describes analysis stages and encoding paths, not independent highlight-selection or engagement results. Optional model ranking can fall back to signal ranking on failure; a completed job does not prove its clips are the best moments. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

### Install

Prepare Rust, Bun, CMake and platform build tools, FFmpeg with libass and yt-dlp. Build the web frontend and run the Rust backend in release mode. The first transcription job downloads the Whisper model. Use Settings to check the detected encoder/dependencies before processing a long recording. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Prepare Rust, Bun, CMake and platform build tools, FFmpeg with libass and yt-dlp. Build the web frontend and run the Rust backend in release mode. The first transcription job downloads the Whisper model. Use Settings to check the detected encoder/dependencies before processing a long recording.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge.git
```


```sh
cd clipforge
```


```sh
cd web
```


```sh
bun install
```


```sh
bun run build
```


```sh
cd ..
```


```sh
cargo run --release
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

1. Open 127.0.0.1:8787, check system/storage readiness and start with a short owned local video.
2. Select blur/crop/fit or the beta facecam layout. Optional AI ranking needs a configured model/provider; local LM Studio/Ollama and hosted routes have different privacy/cost behavior.
3. Watch the exported clips through their reaction endings, inspect word captions and framing, and revise layout. Cached analysis avoids repeating transcription for a re-render.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

- **Hardware:** Documented startup minima: 8 GB RAM and 10 GB free working space; recommended 16 GB RAM/30 GB disk. Linux can use CPU, while Mac requires Apple Silicon. The source does not publish a universal minimum VRAM.
- **Software:** Rust/Bun/CMake and the documented platform build dependencies; FFmpeg/libass and yt-dlp. Whisper large-v3-turbo is about 1.6 GB. macOS needs Apple Silicon; Linux can transcribe on CPU, with CUDA optional.
- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon macOS and Linux are documented; Windows uses WSL2/Linux rather than a native build. Intel Macs are unsupported. Startup requires 8 GB RAM and 10 GB free working space, recommending 16 GB/30 GB. Four CPU cores are a warning threshold, not a hard job minimum.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers the application. Whisper artifacts, FFmpeg builds, model services and source gameplay/streamer material have separate rights. A clip export does not grant permission to repost another creator’s footage.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers the application. Whisper artifacts, FFmpeg builds, model services and source gameplay/streamer material have separate rights. A clip export does not grant permission to repost another creator’s footage.
- **Cost:** Local transcription/rendering with optional separately billed hosted ranking; no required application subscription is documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge/blob/main/Cargo.toml)

### Limitations

Facecam framing is beta and optional ranking is fallible. A local renderer does not make hosted ranking private/offline. Minimum VRAM and a guaranteed processing rate are not documented; large recordings require more storage than the startup threshold. Prototype a parametric reconstruction or editable mechanical prop from an owned scan, with deviations and failed fits explicitly inspected. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Demo & examples

The author publishes several acceptance jobs and scan-deviation percentages. These are first-party tests; valid-solid checks and STEP read-back do not independently prove manufacturing fit or every free-form reconstruction. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Install

Use Python 3.11, a 64-bit OS and an OpenGL 3.2+ driver. Install the editable app and its ai extra in an isolated environment, then open a scan through python -m unmesh. For model control enable the documented Let Claude drive route or run its MCP server in a compatible client. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.11, a 64-bit OS and an OpenGL 3.2+ driver. Install the editable app and its ai extra in an isolated environment, then open a scan through python -m unmesh. For model control enable the documented Let Claude drive route or run its MCP server in a compatible client.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh.git
```


```sh
cd unmesh
```


```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
python -m pip install -e ".[ai]"
```


```sh
python -m unmesh
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Activate the new environment before installing, open an owned scan, align it and choose a reconstruction tolerance appropriate to the measurement evidence.
2. Let the configured model plan and invoke geometry tools, or edit manually, then inspect whole-scan deviation and the history after each group.
3. Export a trial STEP and inspect read-back plus actual critical dimensions. A local “AI reconstruction” button is not by itself evidence of neural inference; the explicit external-agent/MCP path is this guide’s AI scope.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

- **Hardware:** 64-bit system with OpenGL 3.2+; author estimate roughly 2 GB RAM per million scan triangles. Minimum total RAM, dedicated VRAM and disk are not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.11, the source-declared Qt/VTK/OpenCASCADE/Open3D/SolveSpace stack, 64-bit OS and OpenGL 3.2+. A model/MCP client is separate for AI-driven authoring. The README estimates about 2 GB RAM per million scan triangles.
- **Platforms:** Developed/used on Windows; Linux tests run in CI. Apple Silicon macOS CI excludes the main window, which the author says has not been used on a real Mac. Total RAM/VRAM/storage minima are not documented; the triangle-memory estimate scales with input.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

- **Code:** AGPL-3.0
- **Weights:** The complete AGPL-3.0 software grant was reviewed; the README specifies AGPL-3.0-only. Adapted ScanRuler work and CAD/GUI dependencies retain their notices. External-agent/model and scan rights remain separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows AGPL-3.0. The complete AGPL-3.0 software grant was reviewed; the README specifies AGPL-3.0-only. Adapted ScanRuler work and CAD/GUI dependencies retain their notices. External-agent/model and scan rights remain separate.
- **Cost:** Local geometry processing plus separately configured model-agent access. No mandatory Unmesh service account is documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Limitations

The current source explicitly labels the application alpha with rough edges. Scan agreement is not a calibration or fabrication certificate. Trackpad gestures and the Mac GUI remain unresolved, and free-form or difficult parts can remain not done. [Source 1](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh/blob/main/README.md)

## Fosfora neural gesture/depth visuals

Neural rhythm tracking and synchronized visual media · Interactive, immersive & live media · Physical, robotic & kinetic installations · Video, animation & film · Computational art & creative coding

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototype a camera-controlled live projection or VJ scene, mapping a hand landmark to distortion or using neural depth as one input to the image. Ordinary FFT/beat reactivity alone is not the AI claim here.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-14. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Both complete alternative software license files were inspected. This classification relies on the standard MIT grant in LICENSE-MIT; the GitHub-selected Apache file contains altered wording and is not treated as unmodified Apache-2.0. Separate model/runtime/asset terms remain unresolved. [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author publishes effect clips, layered examples and bridge documentation. The demos and first-party compatibility statements have not established independent performance or a reliable end-to-end installation. [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/bridges/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Obtain the official macOS, Windows or Linux release, or build with the documented Rust 1.97+ toolchain. For the neural hand-control route prepare an isolated Python environment, install bridges/requirements-vision.txt, start Fosfora and run the MediaPipe hand bridge. The source commands below are alternatives to a release install, not steps to run both copies. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/bridges/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** The complete LICENSE-MIT grant was reviewed and is the selected software license. The alternative LICENSE-APACHE text has deviations from standard clauses and is not relied upon as an unmodified Apache grant. MediaPipe/YOLO/MiDaS models, NDI runtime, media and other dependencies retain separate terms. Optional Ableton Link changes binary-distribution obligations to GPL and is omitted from prebuilt downloads. Local computation, camera and any chosen output/runtime costs; no mandatory Fosfora subscription is documented. [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/bridges/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. A webcam bridge is a separate process, and Leap Motion/Kinect bridges are explicitly placeholders. A conventional audio-reactive scene is not neural output. Mac system audio requires a loopback route; model inference and visual rendering can compete for resources.

### How it uses AI

A dual-licensed live visualizer exposes MediaPipe/YOLO vision bridges and a MiDaS depth route so learned hand, pose, face or object signals can control its shader and particle layers. [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/bridges/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT)

### Introduction

A dual-licensed live visualizer exposes MediaPipe/YOLO vision bridges and a MiDaS depth route so learned hand, pose, face or object signals can control its shader and particle layers. 1. Activate your isolated Python environment before installing bridge dependencies. Start Fosfora, then start the hand bridge in a separate terminal with webcam access.
2. Press B to open the Binding Matrix and connect a hand X-position source to one visible parameter such as warp intensity. Observe latency and false or missing tracks in the actual lighting.
3. Choose an appropriate output: Spout on Windows, Syphon on macOS, or separately installed NDI. Default local bridge networking differs from another-computer/container access, which requires explicitly enabling Other devices and keeping its access key private.

### How it uses AI

An Apache-licensed Android comic reader offers model-based CPU/GPU/NPU enhancement at image decode, including AnimeVideo and Omni model options. ### Introduction

An Apache-licensed Android comic reader offers model-based CPU/GPU/NPU enhancement at image decode, including AnimeVideo and Omni model options. [Source 1](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/releases/tag/v1.7.0) [Source 3](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/app/build.gradle.kts) [Source 4](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.en.md)

### What it is good for

Compare the readability of owned comic pages on a phone or tablet, without treating enhanced display pixels as an authenticated restoration or a print-ready master. [Source 1](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/releases/tag/v1.7.0) [Source 3](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/app/build.gradle.kts) [Source 4](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.en.md)

### Demo & examples

The source describes model-dependent line enhancement and warns that NPU models can shift colors. No independent before/after fidelity study was established. The release and build file are at 1.7.0 while both reviewed README headers still say 1.6.0. [Source 1](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/releases/tag/v1.7.0) [Source 3](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/app/build.gradle.kts) [Source 4](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.en.md)

### Install

Use the official release APK matching your Android device architecture, or the universal APK when appropriate. The README states Android 8.0+. Install through Android’s package installer after reviewing the release and permissions, then obtain the matching model pack for the selected enhancement backend. The APK asset alone does not settle separate model-pack terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/releases/tag/v1.7.0) [Source 3](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/app/build.gradle.kts) [Source 4](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.en.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official release APK matching your Android device architecture, or the universal APK when appropriate. The README states Android 8.0+. Install through Android’s package installer after reviewing the release and permissions, then obtain the matching model pack for the selected enhancement backend. The APK asset alone does not settle separate model-pack terms.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/releases/tag/v1.7.0) [Source 3](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/app/build.gradle.kts) [Source 4](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.en.md)

1. Open an owned local comic/archive, or configure your own Komga, WebDAV or SMB source. Komga supplies page conversion on that separate server route.
2. Enable the chosen enhancement model and allow first-use GPU/NPU initialization to finish. The author recommends GPU tile size 192, which is a tuning value rather than a universal memory requirement.
3. Compare the enhanced view with the source at an appropriate zoom, especially line shape, text and colors. AnimeVideo is suggested for color pages and Omni-Mini for monochrome; stronger/slower models and NPU color shifts remain device-dependent.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/releases/tag/v1.7.0) [Source 3](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/app/build.gradle.kts) [Source 4](https://github.com/ruzhe85/Komiho/blob/main/README.en.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Android 8.0+ is documented. Release v1.7.0 includes arm64-v8a and universal APK assets. Local archive support and server-format conversion differ; model packs are separately downloaded.
- **Platforms:** Android phones, tablets and large-screen Android devices are documented. Minimum RAM, accelerator capability, total storage and supported GPU/NPU model combinations are not comprehensively specified. There is no documented native macOS, Windows or iOS app route.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. - **Weights:** The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed. Model packs, adapted reader components and external server/media content retain their own notices and terms; the model dependencies have not received a complete rights audit here.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed. Model packs, adapted reader components and external server/media content retain their own notices and terms; the model dependencies have not received a complete rights audit here.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. The reader provides no comic content or permission to access another person’s collection. ### How it uses AI

An MIT PyTorch script learns a damped-harmonic representation of contours extracted from a supplied line image, then exports the fitted path and a progressive drawing animation. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/stakepoolplace/laplace-drawing-dynamic/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/stakepoolplace/laplace-drawing-dynamic/blob/master/8-laplace_drawing_face.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/stakepoolplace/laplace-drawing-dynamic/blob/master/requirements.txt) [Source 4](https://pypi.org/pypi/scipy/1.16.2/json)

1. Activate the new environment before installing. Start with a simple owned line image and inspect contour extraction; automatic stitching can connect separate contours in an unintended order.
2. The script trains per input and chooses CUDA when torch.cuda.is_available(), otherwise CPU. Adjust points, units, epochs and edge thresholds deliberately; the default is 3000 points, 300 units and 6000 epochs, not a performance guarantee.
3. Inspect laplace_drawing.svg, laplace_analysis.png and the animation in a separate output copy. The duration option controls the learned trajectory time, while the animation creates one frame per point at 60 fps; it is not a verified output-length setting. The code can fall back to GIF after FFmpeg failure.
### Hardware & software

### How it uses AI

A GPL Blender add-on invokes the RigNet neural network to infer a skeleton and skinning for the supplied character templates, then brings the rigged object into Blender. A GPL Blender add-on invokes the RigNet neural network to infer a skeleton and skinning for the supplied character templates, then brings the rigged object into Blender. 2. From the RigNet environment, use its documented quick_start.py sample as a separate prerequisite check. The add-on additionally needs quick_start1.py in that folder.
3. Open Blender from the prepared environment, enable the add-on, press N, set the RigNet path and choose a supplied template. Close the Open3D result preview to let import continue, then inspect scale, skinning and deformations before animation.

### Morse haptic messaging · MIT

Explore short group haptic cues for a participatory interaction prototype. The cited implementation is messaging rather than a completed artwork; creative adaptation and performance use remain untested.
The MIT Wear OS app uses Gemini to condense dictated words and suggest replies, then renders short messages as synchronized screen and wrist-vibration Morse patterns.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Wear OS 3+/API 30+ is documented, with up to three watches and Wi-Fi Direct or LAN. Only the online Gemini path is model-based; offline rules and the single-watch simulated demo do not demonstrate neural inference. API access, device support and privacy need a full review.
